A note on the modular representation on the ℤ / 2 \mathbb{Z}/2 -homology groups of the fourth power of real projective space and its application
D - ặng Võ Phúc

Abstract
We write B ​ V h BV_{h} for the classifying space of the elementary Abelian 2-group V h V_{h} of rank h , h, which is homotopy equivalent to the cartesian product of h h copies of ℝ ​ P ∞ . \mathbb{R}P^{\infty}. Its cohomology with ℤ / 2 \mathbb{Z}/2 -coefficients can be identified with the graded unstable algebra P ⊗ h = ℤ / 2 ​ [ t 1 , … , t h ] = { P n ⊗ h } n ≥ 0 P^{\otimes h}=\mathbb{Z}/2[t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}]=\{P^{\otimes h}_{n}\}_{n\geq 0} over the Steenrod ring 𝒜 \mathcal{A} , where grading is by the degree of the homogeneous terms P n ⊗ h P^{\otimes h}_{n} of degree n n in h h generators with the degree of each t i t_{i} being one. Let G ​ L h GL_{h} be the usual general linear group of rank h h over ℤ / 2 . \mathbb{Z}/2. The algebra P ⊗ h P^{\otimes h} admits a left action of 𝒜 \mathcal{A} as well as a right action of G ​ L h . GL_{h}. A central problem of homotopy theory is to determine the structure of the space of G ​ L h GL_{h} -coinvariants, ℤ / 2 ⊗ G ​ L h Ann 𝒜 ¯ H n ( B V h ; ℤ / 2 ) , \mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{h}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}H_{n}(BV_{h};\mathbb{Z}/2), where Ann 𝒜 ¯ ​ H n ​ ( B ​ V h , ℤ / 2 ) = Ann 𝒜 ¯ ​ [ P n ⊗ h ] ∗ {\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}H_{n}(BV_{h};\mathbb{Z}/2)={\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P^{\otimes h}_{n}]^{*} denotes the space of primitive homology classes, considered as a representation of G ​ L h GL_{h} for all n . n. Solving this problem is very difficult and still open for h ≥ 4 . h\geq 4. In this Note, our intent is of studying the dimension of ℤ / 2 ⊗ G ​ L h Ann 𝒜 ¯ [ P n ⊗ h ] ∗ \mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{h}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P^{\otimes h}_{n}]^{*} for the case h = 4 h=4 and the "generic" degrees n n of the form n k , r , s = k ⁡ ( 2 s − 1 ) + r ​ .2 s , n_{k,r,s}=k(2^{s}-1)+r.2^{s}, where k , r , s k,\,r,\,s are positive integers. Applying the results, we investigate the behavior of the Singer cohomological "transfer" of rank 4 . 4. Singer’s transfer is a homomorphism from a certain subquotient of the divided power algebra Γ ⁡ ( a 1 ( 1 ) , … , a h ( 1 ) ) \Gamma(a_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,a_{h}^{(1)}) to mod-2 cohomology groups Ext 𝒜 h , h + ∗ ( ℤ / 2 , ℤ / 2 ) {\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{h,h+*}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2) of the algebra 𝒜 . \mathcal{A}. Singer’s algebraic transfer is one of the relatively efficient tools in determining the cohomology of the Steenrod algebra. Additionally, in higher ranks, by using the results on 𝒜 \mathcal{A} -generators for P ⊗ 5 P^{\otimes 5} and P ⊗ 6 , P^{\otimes 6}, we show in Appendix that the transfer of rank 5 is an isomorphism in som certain degrees of the form n k , r , s n_{k,r,s} , and that the transfer of rank 6 does not detect the non-zero elements h 2 2 ​ g 1 = h 4 ​ P ​ h 2 ∈ Ext 𝒜 6 , 6 + n 6 , 10 , 1 ​ ( ℤ / 2 , ℤ / 2 ) h_{2}^{2}g_{1}=h_{4}Ph_{2}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{6,6+n_{6,10,1}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2) , and D 2 ∈ Ext 𝒜 6 , 6 + n 6 , 10 , 2 ​ ( ℤ / 2 , ℤ / 2 ) . D_{2}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{6,6+n_{6,10,2}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2). Besides, we also probe the behavior of the Singer transfer of ranks 7 and 8 in internal degrees ≤ 15 . \leq 15.



2010 MSC
55Q45, 13A50, 55S10, 55S05, 55T15, 55R12

1
Introduction

The hit problem and its dual.
Let
V_{h}
be an elementary Abelian 2-group of rank
h
, which it will usually be more useful to consider as an
h
-dimensional vector space over the prime field
\mathbb{Z}/2
of characteristic
2
. Denote by
BV_{h}
the classifying space of
V_{h},
which is homotopy equivalent to the cartesian product of
h
copies of the infinite dimensional projective space,
\mathbb{R}P^{\infty}.
Writing
\mathcal{A}
for the Steenrod algebra over
\mathbb{Z}/2,
which is defined to be the algebra of stable cohomology operations for singular cohomology with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}/2.
The algebra
\mathcal{A},
which acts on the cohomology ring of a space, is widely studied by mathematicians whose interests range from Algebraic topology and homotopy theory, to manifold theory, combinatorics, and more. Let
P^{\otimes h}=\mathbb{Z}/2[t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}]=\{P^{\otimes h}_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
be the graded unstable algebra over
\mathcal{A},
where each
t_{i}
has grading one, and
P^{\otimes h}_{n}=\langle\{f\in P^{\otimes h}|\,\mbox{$f$ is a homogeneous polynomial of degree $n$}\}\rangle.
It has been shown (see
[
2
]
) that the ring of endomorphisms of the algebra
P^{\otimes h}
over
\mathcal{A}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/2[M_{h}(\mathbb{Z}/2)],
the ring of the semi-group ring of
h\times h
-matrices over
\mathbb{Z}/2
. So, we can be identified
P^{\otimes h}
with the
\mathbb{Z}/2
-cohomology of
BV_{h}.
As known, the algebra
\mathcal{A}
is generated by the Steenrod squares
Sq^{i}
(
i\geq 0
) and subject to Adem’s relations:
Sq^{i}Sq^{j}=\sum_{0\leq t\leq[i/2]}\binom{j-t-1}{i-2t}Sq^{i+j-t}Sq^{t},\,\,0<i<2j,
where the binomial coefficients are to be interpreted mod 2. The action of
\mathcal{A}
over the polynomial algebra
P^{\otimes h}
is determined by instability axioms. By Cartan’s formula, it suffices to determine
Sq^{i}(t_{j})
and the instability axioms give
Sq^{1}(t_{j})=t_{j}^{2}
while
Sq^{i}(t_{j})=0
if
i>1.
One of the unsolved problems in Algebraic topology is to find minimal sets of
\mathcal{A}
-generators for
P^{\otimes h}.
It is the same as the problem of investigating a basis for the space of the "non-hit" elements
\{QP_{n}^{\otimes h}:=P_{n}^{\otimes h}/\overline{\mathcal{A}}P_{n}^{\otimes h}=(\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes h})_{n}\}_{n\geq 0},
where
\overline{\mathcal{A}}P_{n}^{\otimes h}:=P_{n}^{\otimes h}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes h}
and
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
denotes the set of positive degree elements in
\mathcal{A}.
This is called "hit problem" in literature
[
31
]
and is known for all integers
h
less than or equal to
4
(see
[
31
]
,
[
18
]
,
[
43
,
44
]
). Though much work has been done (see, for instance
[
32
,
33
,
34
,
35
,
36
,
37
,
39
,
40
]
,
[
27
]
,
[
26
]
,
[
47
]
,
[
48
,
49
]
,
[
51
]
), solving the hit problem in the general case seems to be out of reach with the present techniques, even when
h
equal to
5
and in some certain generic degrees. Remarkably, by the works
[
18
]
,
[
47
]
, and
[
52
]
, we need only to study this problem in degrees
n
of the form
k(2^{s}-1)+r.2^{s},
whenever
k,s,r
are positive integers satisfying
\mu(r)<k<h.
Here
\mu(r)
denotes the smallest integer
m
such that
r
can be represented as
r=\sum_{1\leq i\leq m}(2^{u_{i}}-1),
where
u_{i}>0.
Several other aspects of the hit problem has been discovered in the recent works of Ault-Singer
[
3
]
, Inoue
[
13
]
, Janfada-Wood
[
14
]
, Pengelley-William
[
30
]
. The dual of the hit problem is to determine a subring of elements of the Pontrjagin ring
H_{*}(BV_{h};\mathbb{Z}/2)=[P^{\otimes h}]^{*},
which is mapped to zero by all Steenrod squares of positive degrees, frequently denoted by
{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P^{\otimes h}]^{*}.

The Singer cohomological transfer.
We knew that the
\mathbb{Z}/2
-cohomology of the Steenrod algebra is the
E_{2}
-term for the (2-local) Adams spectral sequence, whose abutment is the 2-component of the stable homotopy groups of spheres. More specifically, the
E_{2}
-page of this spectral sequence may be identified as
{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{*,*}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2).
This is what is meant by the aphorism "the cohomology of the Steenrod algebra is an approximation to the stable homotopy groups of spheres".
So, studying the structure of the Adams
E_{2}
-terms becomes very important in homotopy theory. The study of the hit problem and its dual is closely related to describe these
E_{2}
-terms via a cohomological "transfer" of William Singer
[
41
]
. This transfer is constructed as follows. Consider the polynomial ring of one variable,
P^{\otimes 1}=\mathbb{Z}/2[t_{1}].
The canonical
\mathcal{A}
-action on
P^{\otimes 1}
is extended to an
\mathcal{A}
-action on
\mathbb{Z}/2[t_{1},t_{1}^{-1}],
the ring of finite Laurent series with require that
Sq^{n}(t_{1}^{-1})=t_{1}^{n-1}
for all
n\geq 1.
Then,
\mathcal{P}=\langle\{t_{1}^{a}|\ a\geq-1\}\rangle
is
\mathcal{A}
-submodule of
\mathbb{Z}/2[t_{1},t_{1}^{-1}].
One has a short-exact sequence:

(1)

0\to P^{\otimes 1}\xrightarrow{q}\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{\pi}\Sigma^{-1}\mathbb{Z}/2,

where
q
is the inclusion and
\pi
is given by
\pi(t_{1}^{a})=0
if
a\neq-1
and
\pi(t_{1}^{-1})=1.
Denote by
e_{1}
the element in
{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(\Sigma^{-1}\mathbb{Z}/2,P^{\otimes 1}),
which is represented by the cocyle associated to (
1
). For each
i\geq 1,
the short-exact sequence

(2)

0\to P^{\otimes(i+1)}\cong P^{\otimes i}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2}P^{\otimes 1}\xrightarrow{1\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2}q}P^{\otimes i}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2}\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{1\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2}\pi}\Sigma^{-1}P^{\otimes i},

determines a class
(e_{1}\times P^{\otimes i})\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1}(\Sigma^{-1}P^{\otimes i},P^{\otimes(i+1)}).
Then, using the cross product and Yoneda product, we have the element

e_{h}=(e_{1}\times P^{\otimes(h-1)})\circ(e_{1}\times P^{\otimes(h-2)})\circ\cdots\circ(e_{1}\times P^{\otimes 1})\circ e_{1}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{h}(\Sigma^{-h}\mathbb{Z}/2,P^{\otimes h}).

Let
\Delta(e_{1}\times P^{\otimes i}):{\rm Tor}_{h-i}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\Sigma^{-h}P^{\otimes i})\to{\rm Tor}_{h-i-1}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,P^{\otimes(i+1)})
be the connecting homomorphism associated to (
2
). Then, we have a composition of the connecting homomorphisms

\overline{\varphi}^{\mathcal{A}}_{h}=\Delta(e_{1}\times P^{\otimes(h-1)})\circ\Delta(e_{1}\times P^{\otimes(h-2)})\circ\cdots\circ\Delta(e_{1}\times P^{\otimes 1})\circ\Delta(e_{1})

from
{\rm Tor}_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\Sigma^{-h}\mathbb{Z}/2)
to
{\rm Tor}_{0}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,P^{\otimes h})=QP^{\otimes h}=\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes h}
, determined by
\overline{\varphi}_{h}(z)=e_{h}\cap z
for any
z\in{\rm Tor}_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\Sigma^{-h}\mathbb{Z}/2).
Here
\cap
denotes the
cap product
in homology with
\mathbb{Z}/2
-coefficients. The image of
\overline{\varphi}_{h}
is a submodule of the invariant space
[QP^{\otimes h}]^{GL_{h}}.
Hence,
\overline{\varphi}^{\mathcal{A}}_{h}
induces homomorphism
\varphi_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}:{\rm Tor}_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\Sigma^{-h}\mathbb{Z}/2)\to[QP^{\otimes h}]^{GL_{h}}.
Because the supension
\Sigma^{-h}
induces an isomorphism
{\rm Tor}_{h,n}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\Sigma^{-h}\mathbb{Z}/2)\cong{\rm Tor}_{h,h+n}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2),
we have the homomorphism

\varphi_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}:{\rm Tor}_{h,h+n}^{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)\to[QP^{\otimes h}_{n}]^{GL_{h}}.

Then its dual
Tr_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{h}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n}^{\otimes h}]^{*}\to{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{h,h+n}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)
is called the
cohomological transfer
(or
algebraic transfer
) of rank
h
. This transfer can be seen as an algebraic formulation of a stable transfer
B(V_{h})_{+}^{S}\to S^{0}.
It has been shown that
Tr_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}
is an isomorphism for
h
less than or equal to
3
(see the work of Singer
[
41
]
and of Boardman
[
4
]
). In addition, the "total" algebraic transfer
\{Tr_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}\}_{h\geq 0}
is an algebraic homomorphism (see also
[
41
]
). These events show that
Tr_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}
is highly nontrivial and should be an useful tool to study the mysterious Ext groups. Specifically, Singer
[
41
]
states the following yet left-open:
Tr_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}
is a monomorphism, for all positive integers
h
. One should note that by the works of Minami
[
23
,
24
]
, hit problems and this transfer also make a useful contribution for surveying the Kervaire invariant one problem. A review of known results on this problem is necessary for the interested readers. The Kervaire invariant was first introduced by Browder’s work
[
6
]
, where he shows that the classes
h_{j}^{2}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{2,2^{j+1}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)
are the permanent cycles in the classical Adams spectral sequence at the prime 2
[
1
]
, if and only if smooth framed manifolds of Kervaire invariant one exist only in dimensions
2^{j+1}-2.
The hypothetical element in stable
2^{j+1}-2
-stem,
\pi^{S}_{2^{j+1}-2},
represented by such a framed manifold is denoted by
\theta_{j}.
These
\theta_{j}
are known to be exist for
j\leq 5
(see, for instance Lin, and Mahowald
[
19
]
). Many open issues in Algebraic and Differential topology depend on knowing whether or not the Kervaire invariant one elements
\theta_{j}
for
j\geq 6.
In 2016, Hill, Hopkins, and Ravenel
[
17
]
indicated that these elements do not exist for
j\geq 7
. The case
j=6
is still no answer. Thus, smooth framed manifolds of Kervaire invariant one therefore exist only in dimensions
2,6,14,30,62,
and possibly
126.

The lambda algebra.
Beyond Singer’s algebraic transfer, the mod 2 lambda algebra
\Lambda
of six authors
[
5
]
is also an important tool for computing the cohomology of
\mathcal{A}.
This algebra considered as the
E_{1}
-term of the Adams spectral sequence converging to the 2-component of the stable homotopy groups of spheres. (Note that a spectral sequence arises from a filtration of the dual chain complex and it provides an alternative way to determine the cohomology of the dual chain complex.) Let us briefly review the related concepts of this algebra. Firstly, we know that
\Lambda
is an associative differential bigraded algebra with generators
\lambda_{n}\in\Lambda^{1,n}
(
n\geq 0
) and the Adem relations

(3)

\lambda_{i}\lambda_{2i+n+1}=\sum_{j\geq 0}\binom{n-j-1}{j}\lambda_{i+n-j}\lambda_{2i+1+j}\ \ \ (i\geq 0,\ n\geq 0)

with differential

(4)

\delta(\lambda_{n-1})=\sum_{j\geq 1}\binom{n-j-1}{j}\lambda_{n-j-1}\lambda_{j-1}\ \ \ (n\geq 1),

where the binomial coefficients
\binom{n-j-1}{j}
modulo
2.
Now, for non-negative integers
j_{1},\ldots,j_{h},
the element
\lambda_{j_{1}}\ldots\lambda_{j_{h}}
in
\Lambda
is said to be
a monomial of length
h
. Then, by the relations (
3
), the
\mathbb{Z}/2
-vector subspace
\Lambda^{h,n}=\langle\{\lambda_{j_{1}}\ldots\lambda_{j_{h}}|\ j_{m}\geq 0,\ 1\leq m\leq h,\ \sum_{1\leq m\leq h}j_{m}=n\}\rangle
of
\Lambda
has an additive basis consisting of all admissible monomials of length
h
(i.e., those of the form
\lambda_{j_{1}}\ldots\lambda_{j_{h}}
where
j_{i}\leq 2j_{i+1}
for all
0<i<h
.) Moreover, it is known that there is an endomorphism
Sq^{0}
of
\Lambda,
determined by
Sq^{0}(\lambda_{j_{1}}\ldots\lambda_{j_{h}})=\lambda_{2j_{1}+1}\ldots\lambda_{2j_{h}+1}.
Further, this
Sq^{0}
respects the relations in (
3
) and commutes with the differential
\delta
in (
4
). So, it induces the first Steenrod operation in Ext groups (see Palmieri
[
29
]
for more details). It is known that in standard notation,
H_{*}(V_{h};\mathbb{Z}/2)=H_{*}(BV_{h};\mathbb{Z}/2)=[P^{\otimes h}]^{*}=\Gamma(x_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,x_{h}^{(1)}),
the divided polynomial algebra on
V_{h}
over
\mathbb{Z}/2,
generated by
x_{1}^{(1)},\ldots,x_{h}^{(1)},
where
x_{i}^{(1)}
is linear dual to
t_{i}.
The (right) action of the Steenrod algebra on
[P^{\otimes h}]^{*}
is determined by the usual Cartan formula and for
k>0,

(x^{(n)}_{i})Sq^{k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\binom{n-k}{k}x^{(n-k)}&\mbox{if $2k<n$},\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

This shows that
[P^{\otimes h}]^{*}
has a natural right
\mathcal{A}
-module structure. An interesting
\mathbb{Z}/2
-linear map
\psi_{h}
from
[P^{\otimes h}_{n}]^{*}
to
\Lambda^{h,n},
established by Ch
\ohorn
n and Hà
[
10
]
, is determined by
\psi_{h}(x_{1}^{(j_{1})}\ldots x_{h}^{(j_{h})})=\lambda_{j_{h}}
if
h=1,
and
\psi_{h}(x_{1}^{(j_{1})}\ldots x_{h}^{(j_{h})})=\sum_{k\geq j_{h}}\psi_{h-1}(((x_{1}^{(j_{1})}\ldots x_{h-1}^{(j_{h-1})}))Sq^{k-j_{h}})\lambda_{k}
otherwise. The authors indicated that this homomorphism can be considered as a representation in the algebra
\Lambda
of the algebraic transfer, and that if
\zeta\in{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n}^{\otimes h}]^{*},
then the image of
\zeta
under
\psi_{h}
is a cycle in
\Lambda^{h,n}
and is a representative of the image of the class
[\zeta]
under
Tr_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}.

The structure of the coinvariant space
\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{h}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n}^{\otimes h}]^{*}
and the behavior of the Singer transfer have been proved surprisingly difficult by many researchers; for instance the interested reader can see the works by Boardman
[
4
]
, Ch
\ohorn
n and Hà
[
9
,
10
]
, Crabb-Hubbuck
[
11
]
, Crossley
[
12
]
, Hà
[
15
]
, H
\uhorn
ng
[
16
]
, Minami
[
23
]
, Nam
[
28
]
, the present writer
[
33
,
34
,
36
,
37
,
38
,
39
,
40
]
, Sum
[
46
,
47
]
, etc. These problems are still unknown for
h\geq 4.
In the rank 4 case, by the work of Sum
[
43
]
, it is sufficient to study them in the "generic degrees"
n
of the following forms:

(i)

2^{s+1}-i
for
1\leq i\leq 3,

(ii)

2^{s+m+1}+2^{s+1}-3,

(iii)

2^{s+m}+2^{s}-2,

(iv)

2^{s+m+u}+2^{s+m}+2^{s}-3,

for any positive integers
s,\,m,\,u
. It is easy to see that all these degrees
n
can be written as
n=k(2^{s}-1)+r.2^{s},
where
k,\,r,\,s
are positive integers and
0<\mu(r)<k<4.
The information on the behavior of
Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}
in degrees of the form (i) is known by Sum
[
46
]
. The remaining cases have been partially studied by the present author
[
38
,
40
]
.

In this work, we will continue to probe the problems above for the case of the generic degree
n=2^{s+m}+2^{s}-2
with
m\in\{2,4\}
and
s
an arbitrary positive integer. Thence, we show that the Singer transfer is an isomorphism in bidegree
(4,4+n).
Moreover, in Appendix, basing the works by Bruner
[
7
]
, Mothebe, and Uys
[
25
]
, Moetele, and Mothebe
[
27
]
, Mothebe, Kaelo, and Ramatebele
[
26
]
, Lin
[
20
]
, Sum
[
43
,
44
,
45
,
47
]
, Walker, and Wood
[
51
]
, Tin
[
48
,
49
]
, and our previous works
[
34
,
35
,
36
]
, we would like to survey the behavior of the transfer of ranks
>4
in some internal degrees. Most notably, in the rank 6 case, we shall show that the transfer
Tr_{6}^{\mathcal{A}}
does not detect the non-zero elements:

\begin{array}[]{ll}&h_{1}Ph_{1}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{6,6+10}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2),\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ h_{0}Ph_{2}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{6,6+11}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2),\\
&h_{2}^{2}g_{1}=h_{4}Ph_{2}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{6,6+26}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2),\ D_{2}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{6,6+58}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2),\end{array}

and that in the rank 7 case, the transfer
Tr_{7}^{\mathcal{A}}
does not detect the non-zero element
h_{0}^{2}Ph_{2}=h_{1}^{2}Ph_{1}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{7,7+11}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2).
The results for the elements
h_{1}Ph_{1},\,h_{0}Ph_{2},
and
h_{0}^{2}Ph_{2}=h_{1}^{2}Ph_{1}
were also proved by Ch
\ohorn
n, and Hà
[
9
,
10
]
using other techniques. In addition, in ranks
h=6,\,7,\,8,
we refute the results by Moetele, and Mothebe
[
27
]
on the dimensions of
QP_{13}^{\otimes h}
, from which we also indicate that the Singer transfer is an isomorphism in bidegree
(h,h+13).
Our method is based on techniques of the hit problem of four variables in
[
43
,
44
]
and a representation in the lambda algebra of
Tr_{h}^{\mathcal{A}}
. The techniques can be applied to study Singer’s transfer of a higher rank in some certain generic degrees. Our approach is different from the ones of Singer
[
41
]
, H
\uhorn
ng
[
16
]
, Ch
\ohorn
n, and Hà
[
9
,
10
]
, Nam
[
28
]
in studying the image of the cohomological transfer.

2
Main results

As mentioned above, and more precisely, we shall explicitly determine the structure of the coinvariants
\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}
at the generic degrees
n=5.2^{s}-2
and
17.2^{s}-2
for every positive integer
s.
Then, we claim that the fourth cohomological transfer is an isomorphism in these internal degrees. The content of the present Note subsection-wise can be described as follows.

2.1
The degree
n_{s}:=5.2^{s}-2

Notice that the Kameko homomorphism

\begin{array}[]{ll}(\widetilde{Sq^{0}_{*}})_{n_{s}}:QP^{\otimes 4}_{n_{s}}&\longrightarrow QP^{\otimes 4}_{\frac{n_{s}-4}{2}}.\\
\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{[}\prod_{1\leq j\leq 4}t_{j}^{a_{j}}\mbox{]}&\longmapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mbox{[}\prod_{1\leq j\leq 4}t_{j}^{\frac{a_{j}-1}{2}}\mbox{]}&\text{if $a_{j}$ odd, for all $j$},\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

is an epimorphism of
\mathbb{Z}/2[GL_{4}]
-modules, hence we have an isomorphism

QP^{\otimes 4}_{n_{s}}\cong{\rm Ker}((\widetilde{Sq^{0}_{*}})_{n_{s}})\bigoplus QP^{\otimes 4}_{\frac{n_{s}-4}{2}},

which implies that

(5)

\dim(\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n_{s}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*})\leq\dim([{\rm Ker}((\widetilde{Sq^{0}_{*}})_{n_{s}})]^{GL_{4}})+\dim(\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{\frac{n_{s}-4}{2}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}).

According to Lin
[
20
]
, we deduce that

(6)

{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n_{s}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\langle h_{1}^{2}h_{2}^{2}\rangle=0&\mbox{if $s=1$},\\[2.84526pt]
\langle h_{1}^{3}h_{4},f_{0}\rangle=\langle h_{0}^{2}h_{2}h_{4},f_{0}\rangle&\mbox{if $s=2$},\\[2.84526pt]
\langle h_{0}^{2}h_{3}h_{5},e_{1}\rangle&\mbox{if $s=3$},\\[2.84526pt]
\langle h_{0}^{2}h_{s}h_{s+2}\rangle&\mbox{if $s\geq 4$}.\end{array}\right.

By using these data and an admissible basis of
{\rm Ker}((\widetilde{Sq^{0}_{*}})_{n_{s}})
in
[
43
,
44
]
, we obtain the following results.

The case
s=1
. We have

Theorem 2.1.1
.

The coinvariant space
\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n_{1}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}
is trivial.

In order to prove the theorem, we use a basis of
{\rm Ker}((\widetilde{Sq^{0}_{*}})_{n_{1}})
and show that
{\rm Ker}((\widetilde{Sq^{0}_{*}})_{n_{1}})
is trivial. At the same time, combining the inequality (
5
) and a theorem of Sum
[
46
]
that the coinvariant space
(\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{\frac{n_{1}-4}{2}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*})
is trivial. Note that, this result has also been investigated in
[
8
]
, in a different way. Now, by Theorem
4.2.1
and the equality (
6
), it may be concluded that

Corollary 2.1.2
.

The rank 4 algebraic transfer

Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n_{1}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}\to{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)

is a trivial isomorphism.

Moreover, we have the following remarks.

Remark 2.1.3
.

We consider the generic degree
12.2^{s}-4
with
s
an arbitrary positive integer. It is easy to see that
\mu(12.2^{s}-4)=4
for all
s>0.
(Recall that for each positive integer
n,
by
\mu(n)
one means the smallest
number
k
for which it is possible to write
n=\sum_{1\leq i\leq k}(2^{d_{i}}-1),
in which
d_{i}>0.
) Then, by Kameko’s Theorem
[
18
]
, it follows that the iterated Kameko map

(\widetilde{Sq^{0}_{*}})^{s}_{12.2^{s}-4}:QP^{\otimes 4}_{12.2^{s}-4}\longrightarrow QP^{\otimes 4}_{8}

is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}/2GL_{4}
-modules, for any
s\geq 0.
Combining this fact and Theorem
4.2.1
, we conclude that the coinvariants
\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{12.2^{s}-4}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}
is trivial for every non-negative integer
s.
This also explained in
[
8
]
, in another way. On the other hand, by calculations of Lin
[
20
]
, clearly
{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,12.2^{s}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)=\mathbb{Z}/2.g_{s}
for all
s>0.
Therefore, the transfer homomorphism

Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{12.2^{s}-4}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}\to{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,12.2^{s}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)

is a monomorphism, but not an isomorphism for arbitrary
s>0.

s=2
. It is known, from a theorem of Sum

]
, that
\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{\frac{n_{2}-4}{2}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}
is generated by
[x_{1}^{(0)}x_{2}^{(0)}x_{3}^{(0)}x_{4}^{(7)}].

.

The fourth cohomological transfer is a isomorphism in the internal degree

Theorem 2.2.1
.

The space
\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n^{\prime}_{1}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}
is 1-dimensional and generated by
[\overline{\zeta}].

An effective approach, based on the inequality (
7
) coupled with a result of Sum
[
46
]
and the calculations of the invariant space
[{\rm Ker}_{n^{\prime}_{1}}]^{GL_{4}}
, is presented in the proof of the theorem. Now, it is easy to see that
\overline{d}_{1}=\lambda_{7}^{2}\lambda_{5}\lambda_{13}+\lambda_{7}^{2}\lambda_{9}^{2}+\lambda_{7}\lambda_{11}\lambda_{9}\lambda_{5}+\lambda_{15}\lambda_{3}\lambda_{11}\lambda_{3}\in\Lambda^{4,n^{\prime}_{1}}
is a cycle in
\Lambda
and is a representative of the non-zero element
d_{1}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2).
Then, using Theorem
2.2.1
and a representation in
\Lambda
of
Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}},
we deduce that
d_{1}=Tr_{4}([\overline{\zeta}])=[\psi_{4}(\overline{\zeta})].
This implies that
d_{1}
is in the image of
Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}.
Combining this with Theorem
2.2.1
and the fact that
{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{1}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)
has dimension one, we immediately obtain

Corollary 2.2.2
.

The transfer
Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}
is an isomorphism when acting on
\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n^{\prime}_{1}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}.

The case
s=2
. Using the inequality (
7
) and a result in Sum
[
46
]
, we have

Theorem 2.2.3
.

The coinvariant space
\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n^{\prime}_{2}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}
is the
\mathbb{Z}/2
-vector space of 1 dimension with the basis
\{[x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(1)}x_{3}^{(1)}x_{4}^{(63)}]\}.

We observe that
\lambda_{1}\in\Lambda^{1,1}
and
\lambda_{63}\in\Lambda^{1,63}
are the cycles in
\Lambda
and
[\lambda_{1}]=h_{1}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1,2}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)
and
[\lambda_{63}]=h_{6}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{1,64}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2).
So, from Theorem
2.2.3
and the representation of the fourth transfer homomorphism over the algebra
\Lambda,
we claim that the cycle
\lambda_{1}^{3}\lambda_{63}=\psi_{4}(x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(1)}x_{3}^{(1)}x_{4}^{(63)})
in
\Lambda
is a representative of the non-zero element
h_{1}^{3}h_{6}=h_{0}^{2}h_{2}h_{6}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2).
This shows that
h_{1}^{3}h_{6}\in{\rm Im}(Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}})
and so, the following corollary is immediate from the fact that
{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)
is 1-dimensional.

Corollary 2.2.4
.

The algebraic transfer
Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n^{\prime}_{2}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}\to{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)
is an isomorphism.

The case
s\geq 3
. The following theorem is proved by using the equality (
7
) and our result in
[
38
]
.

Theorem 2.2.5
.

Let
s
be a positive integer such that
s\geq 3.
Then, we have

\dim\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n^{\prime}_{s}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\mbox{if $s=4$},\\
2&\mbox{if $s\neq 4$}.\end{array}\right.

Furthermore,

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n^{\prime}_{s}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\langle[x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(7)}x_{3}^{(63)}x_{4}^{(63)}],[x_{1}^{(0)}x_{2}^{(0)}x_{3}^{(7)}x_{4}^{(127)}]\rangle&\mbox{if $s=3$},\\[2.84526pt]
\langle[x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(15)}x_{3}^{(127)}x_{4}^{(127)}]\rangle&\mbox{if $s=4$},\\[2.84526pt]
\langle[x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(2^{s-1}-1)}x_{3}^{(2^{s-1}-1)}x_{4}^{(2^{s+4}-1)}],[x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(2^{s}-1)}x_{3}^{(2^{s+3}-1)}x_{4}^{(2^{s+3}-1)}]\rangle&\mbox{if $s\geq 5$}.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

From this theorem and the representation in
\Lambda
of the rank 4 transfer, it may be concluded that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&[\psi_{4}(x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(7)}x_{3}^{(63)}x_{4}^{(63)})]=[\lambda_{1}\lambda_{7}\lambda_{63}^{2}]\\
&=Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}([x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(7)}x_{3}^{(63)}x_{4}^{(63)}])=h_{1}h_{3}h_{6}^{2}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{3}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2),\\
&[\psi_{4}(x_{1}^{(0)}x_{2}^{(0)}x_{3}^{(7)}x_{4}^{(127)})]=[\lambda_{0}\lambda_{7}\lambda_{127}^{2}]\\
&=Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}([x_{1}^{(0)}x_{2}^{(0)}x_{3}^{(7)}x_{4}^{(127)}])=h_{0}^{2}h_{3}h_{7}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{3}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2),\\
&[\psi_{4}(x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(15)}x_{3}^{(127)}x_{4}^{(127)})]=[\lambda_{1}\lambda_{15}\lambda_{127}^{2}]\\
&=Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}([x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(15)}x_{3}^{(127)}x_{4}^{(127)}])=h_{1}h_{4}h_{7}^{3}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{4}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2),\\
&[\psi_{4}(x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(2^{s-1}-1)}x_{3}^{(2^{s-1}-1)}x_{4}^{(2^{s+4}-1)})]=[\lambda_{1}\lambda^{2}_{2^{s-1}-1}\lambda_{2^{s+4}-1}]\\
&=Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}([x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(2^{s-1}-1)}x_{3}^{(2^{s-1}-1)}x_{4}^{(2^{s+4}-1)}])=h_{1}h_{s-1}^{2}h_{s+4}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{s}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)\ \mbox{for $s\geq 5$}\\
&[\psi_{4}(x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(2^{s}-1)}x_{3}^{(2^{s+3}-1)}x_{4}^{(2^{s+3}-1)})]=[\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2^{s}-1}\lambda_{2^{s+3}-1}^{2}]\\
&=Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}([x_{1}^{(1)}x_{2}^{(2^{s}-1)}x_{3}^{(2^{s+3}-1)}x_{4}^{(2^{s+3}-1)}])=h_{1}h_{s}h_{s+3}^{2}\in{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{s}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)\ \mbox{for $s\geq 5$}.\end{array}

Combining these with the fact that

\dim{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{s}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\mbox{if $s=4$},\\
2&\mbox{if $s=3$ and $s\geq 5$},\end{array}\right.

we have immediately

Corollary 2.2.6
.

For each
s>2,
the cohomological transfer

Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{Z}/2\otimes_{GL_{4}}{\rm Ann}_{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}[P_{n^{\prime}_{s}}^{\otimes 4}]^{*}\to{\rm Ext}_{\mathcal{A}}^{4,4+n^{\prime}_{s}}(\mathbb{Z}/2,\mathbb{Z}/2)

is also an isomorphism.

Thus, Corollaries
2.1.2
-
2.2.6
confirmed Singer’s conjecture that
Tr_{4}^{\mathcal{A}}
is a monomorphism in all the internal degrees
q.2^{s}-2
for
q\in\{5,17\}
and all positive integers
s.
Moreover, motivated by the data in this paper, we propose the following.

Conjecture 2.2.7
.

Let us considere degrees
2^{s+m}+2^{s}-2
, where
s,\,m
are positive integers. Then, Singer’s conjecture for the rank 4 transfer holds in these internal degrees.

So, by our previous results
[
38
,
40
]
and the work of Sum
[
46
]
, we see that if this conjecture is true then the fourth algebraic transfer is a monomorphism for all degrees
n.
This means that Singer’s conjecture is true for cohomological degrees less than or equal to 4.

3
Proofs of main results

We first provide some definitions, which will be used in proofs of our main results.

Definition 3.1
.

Let us consider the polynomials
u
and
v
in
P^{\otimes h}_{n}.
We say that
u\equiv v
if and only if
(u+v)
is
\mathcal{A}
-decomposable (or "hit").

The readers can see that the binary relation "
\equiv
" on
P^{\otimes h}_{n}
is an equivalence relation.

Definition 3.2
.

For each
1\leq j\leq h,
we define the
\mathbb{Z}/2
-linear map

\theta_{j}:V_{h}\cong\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}\rangle\to V_{h}\cong\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}\rangle

by subsituting
\theta_{j}(t_{j})=t_{j+1},

\theta_{j}(t_{j+1})=t_{j},

\theta_{j}(t_{i})=t_{i},
for
i\neq j,\,j+1,

1\leq j<4,
and
\theta_{h}(t_{1})=t_{1}+t_{2},

\theta_{4}(t_{i})=t_{i},
for
1<i\leq h.

From this definition, the group
GL_{h}\cong GL(V_{h})
is generated by
\theta_{j},
for
1\leq j\leq h,
and the symmetric group
S_{h}
is generated by
\theta_{j}
for
1\leq j<h.
Further,
\theta_{j}
induces a homomorphism of
\mathcal{A}
-algebras which is also denoted by
\theta_{j}:P^{\otimes h}\to P^{\otimes h}.
This together with Definition
3.1
say that a class
[u]\in QP_{n}^{\otimes h}
is an
S_{h}
-invariant (resp.
GL_{h}
-invariant) if and only if
\theta_{j}(u)\equiv u
for
1\leq j<h
(resp.
1\leq j\leq h
).

Now, in what follows: for any admissible monomials
{\rm adm}_{1},\ldots,{\rm adm}_{k}
in
P^{\otimes 4}_{n}
and for a subgroup
\mathscr{G}
of
GL_{4},
let us denote
\mathscr{G}({\rm adm}_{1},\ldots,{\rm adm}_{k})
the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathscr{G}
-submodule of
{\rm Ker}_{n}:={\rm Ker}((\widetilde{Sq^{0}_{*}})_{n})
generated by the set
\{[{\rm adm}_{j}]:\,1\leq j\leq k\}.

We have an isomorphism

{\rm Ker}_{n}\cong\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n}}\bigoplus\widehat{{\rm Ker}_{n}},

where

\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n}}&:=\langle\{[t_{1}^{a_{1}}t_{2}^{a_{2}}t_{3}^{a_{3}}t_{4}^{a_{4}}]\in{\rm Ker}_{n}:\,a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}=0\}\rangle,\\
\widehat{{\rm Ker}_{n}}&:=\langle\{[t_{1}^{a_{1}}t_{2}^{a_{2}}t_{3}^{a_{3}}t_{4}^{a_{4}}]\in{\rm Ker}_{n}:\,a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4}\neq 0\}\rangle\end{array}

are
\mathbb{Z}/2
-subspaces of
{\rm Ker}_{n}.

3.1
The degree
n_{s}=5.2^{s}-2

In order to prove Theorems
4.2.1
,
2.1.4
,
2.1.6
and
2.1.8
, we need to some related results.

The case
s=1
. According to Sum
[
43
,
44
]
,
\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n_{1}}}
has a basis consisting of all the classes represented by the admissible monomials
{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}
for all
1\leq j\leq 42,
where

{\rm adm}_{1,\,1}=t_{3}t_{4}^{7}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,2}=t_{3}^{7}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,3}=t_{2}t_{4}^{7}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,4}=t_{2}t_{3}^{7}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,5}=t_{2}^{7}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,6}=t_{2}^{7}t_{3}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,7}=t_{1}t_{4}^{7}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,8}=t_{1}t_{3}^{7}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,9}=t_{1}t_{2}^{7}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,10}=t_{1}^{7}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,11}=t_{1}^{7}t_{3}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,12}=t_{1}^{7}t_{2}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,13}=t_{3}^{3}t_{4}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,14}=t_{2}^{3}t_{4}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,15}=t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,16}=t_{1}^{3}t_{4}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,17}=t_{1}^{3}t_{3}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,18}=t_{1}^{3}t_{2}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,19}=t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}^{6}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,20}=t_{2}t_{3}^{6}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,21}=t_{1}t_{3}t_{4}^{6}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,22}=t_{1}t_{3}^{6}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,23}=t_{1}t_{2}t_{4}^{6}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,24}=t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}^{6}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,25}=t_{1}t_{2}^{6}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,26}=t_{1}t_{2}^{6}t_{3}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,27}=t_{2}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,28}=t_{1}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,29}=t_{1}t_{2}^{2}t_{4}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,30}=t_{1}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{5}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,31}=t_{2}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}^{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,32}=t_{2}^{3}t_{3}t_{4}^{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,33}=t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{4}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,34}=t_{1}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}^{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,35}=t_{1}t_{2}^{3}t_{4}^{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,36}=t_{1}t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,37}=t_{1}^{3}t_{3}t_{4}^{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,38}=t_{1}^{3}t_{2}t_{4}^{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,39}=t_{1}^{3}t_{2}t_{3}^{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,40}=t_{1}^{3}t_{3}^{4}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,41}=t_{1}^{3}t_{2}^{4}t_{4}
,

{\rm adm}_{1,\,42}=t_{1}^{3}t_{2}^{4}t_{3}
.

From the admissible basis above, by a simple computation, we see that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,1})=\langle\{[{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}]:\,1\leq j\leq 12\}\rangle,\\
&S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,13})=\langle\{[{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}]:\,13\leq j\leq 18]\}\rangle,\\
&S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,19},\ {\rm adm}_{1,\,27})=\langle\{[{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}]:\,19\leq j\leq 42\}\rangle\\
\end{array}

are
S_{4}
-submodules of
\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n_{1}}}.
Therefore, we have an isomorphism

\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n_{1}}}\cong S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,1})\bigoplus S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,13})\bigoplus S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,19},\ {\rm adm}_{1,\,27}).

Lemma 3.1.1
.

The following statements are true:

i)

[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,1})]^{S_{4}}=\langle[p_{1,\,1}]\rangle
with
p_{1,\,1}:=\sum_{1\leq j\leq 12}{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}.

ii)

[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,13})]^{S_{4}}=\langle[p_{1,\,2}]\rangle
with
p_{1,\,2}:=\sum_{13\leq j\leq 18}{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}.

iii)

[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,19},\ {\rm adm}_{1,\,27})]^{S_{4}}=\langle[p_{1,\,3}]\rangle
with
p_{1,\,3}:=\sum_{\mathbb{J}\setminus\{27,\ldots,31,34,35,36\}}{\rm adm}_{1,\,j},

where the set
\mathbb{J}:=\{19,20,\ldots,42\}.

Proof.

We prove Part iii) in detail. The others can be proved by the similar computations.

It is easy to see that the set
\{[{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}]:\,19\leq j\leq 42\}
is a basis of
S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,19},\ {\rm adm}_{1,\,27}).
Suppose that
[f]\in[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,19},\ {\rm adm}_{1,\,27})]^{S_{4}},
then
f\equiv\sum_{19\leq j\leq 42}\gamma_{j}{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}
in which
\gamma_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}/2.
Acting the homomorphisms
\theta_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq 3
on both sides of this equality, we get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{1}(f)&\equiv\gamma_{21}{\rm adm}_{1,\,19}+\gamma_{22}{\rm adm}_{1,\,20}+\gamma_{19}{\rm adm}_{1,\,21}+\gamma_{20}{\rm adm}_{1,\,22}+\gamma_{23}{\rm adm}_{1,\,23}\\
&+\gamma_{24}{\rm adm}_{1,\,24}+\gamma_{28}{\rm adm}_{1,\,27}+\gamma_{27}{\rm adm}_{1,\,28}+\gamma_{34}{\rm adm}_{1,\,34}+\gamma_{37}{\rm adm}_{1,\,32}\\
&+\gamma_{40}{\rm adm}_{1,\,33}+\gamma_{31}{\rm adm}_{1,\,34}+\gamma_{38}{\rm adm}_{1,\,35}+\gamma_{39}{\rm adm}_{1,\,36}+\gamma_{32}{\rm adm}_{1,\,37}\\
&+\gamma_{35}{\rm adm}_{1,\,38}+\gamma_{36}{\rm adm}_{1,\,39}+\gamma_{33}{\rm adm}_{1,\,40}+\gamma_{25}t_{1}^{6}t_{2}t_{4}+\gamma_{26}t_{1}^{6}t_{2}t_{3}\\
&+\gamma_{29}t_{1}^{2}t_{2}t_{4}^{5}+\gamma_{30}t_{1}^{2}t_{2}t_{3}^{5}+\gamma_{41}t_{1}^{4}t_{2}^{3}t_{4}+\gamma_{42}t_{1}^{4}t_{2}^{3}t_{3},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{2}(f)&\equiv\gamma_{19}{\rm adm}_{1,\,19}+\gamma_{23}{\rm adm}_{1,\,21}+\gamma_{25}{\rm adm}_{1,\,22}+\gamma_{21}{\rm adm}_{1,\,23}+\gamma_{26}{\rm adm}_{1,\,24}\\
&+\gamma_{22}{\rm adm}_{1,\,25}+\gamma_{24}{\rm adm}_{1,\,26}+\gamma_{29}{\rm adm}_{1,\,28}+\gamma_{28}{\rm adm}_{1,\,29}+\gamma_{32}{\rm adm}_{1,\,31}\\
&+\gamma_{31}{\rm adm}_{1,\,32}+\gamma_{35}{\rm adm}_{1,\,34}+\gamma_{34}{\rm adm}_{1,\,35}+\gamma_{38}{\rm adm}_{1,\,37}+\gamma_{37}{\rm adm}_{1,\,38}\\
&+\gamma_{42}{\rm adm}_{1,\,39}+\gamma_{41}{\rm adm}_{1,\,40}+\gamma_{40}{\rm adm}_{1,\,41}+\gamma_{39}{\rm adm}_{1,\,42}+\gamma_{20}t_{2}^{6}t_{3}t_{4}\\
&+\gamma_{27}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}t_{4}^{5}+\gamma_{30}t_{1}t_{2}^{5}t_{3}^{2}+\gamma_{33}t_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}+\gamma_{36}t_{1}t_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{3},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{3}(f)&\equiv\gamma_{20}{\rm adm}_{1,\,19}+\gamma_{19}{\rm adm}_{1,\,20}+\gamma_{22}{\rm adm}_{1,\,21}+\gamma_{21}{\rm adm}_{1,\,22}+\gamma_{24}{\rm adm}_{1,\,23}\\
&+\gamma_{23}{\rm adm}_{1,\,24}+\gamma_{26}{\rm adm}_{1,\,25}+\gamma_{25}{\rm adm}_{1,\,26}+\gamma_{30}{\rm adm}_{1,\,29}+\gamma_{29}{\rm adm}_{1,\,30}\\
&+\gamma_{33}{\rm adm}_{1,\,32}+\gamma_{32}{\rm adm}_{1,\,33}+\gamma_{36}{\rm adm}_{1,\,35}+\gamma_{35}{\rm adm}_{1,\,36}+\gamma_{40}{\rm adm}_{1,\,37}\\
&+\gamma_{39}{\rm adm}_{1,\,38}+\gamma_{38}{\rm adm}_{1,\,39}+\gamma_{37}{\rm adm}_{1,\,40}+\gamma_{42}{\rm adm}_{1,\,41}+\gamma_{41}{\rm adm}_{1,\,42}\\
&+\gamma_{27}t_{2}t_{3}^{5}t_{4}^{2}+\gamma_{28}t_{1}t_{3}^{5}t_{4}^{2}+\gamma_{31}t_{2}t_{3}^{4}t_{4}^{3}+\gamma_{34}t_{1}t_{3}^{4}t_{4}^{3}.\end{array}

As well known, the (left) action of Steenrod algebra on
P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{s}}
is determined by

Sq^{n}(t_{j})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}t_{j}&\mbox{if}&n=0,\\
t_{j}^{2}&\mbox{if}&n=1,\\
0&\mbox{if}&n>1,\end{array}\right.

and Cartan’s formula
Sq^{n}(fg)=\sum_{k+\ell=n}Sq^{k}(f)Sq^{\ell}(g)
for all
f,\,g\in P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{s}}.
Using this action, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{2}t_{3}^{4}t_{4}^{3}&=Sq^{2}(t_{2}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}^{3})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,27}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{2}t_{3}^{5}t_{4}^{2}&=Sq^{2}(t_{2}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,31}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{2}^{2}t_{3}t_{4}^{5}&=Sq^{1}(t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}^{5})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,19}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,27}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}&=Sq^{1}(t_{2}t_{3}^{5}t_{4})+Sq^{2}(t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}+t_{2}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,20}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,31}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{2}^{6}t_{3}t_{4}&=Sq^{1}(t_{2}^{5}t_{3}t_{4})+Sq^{2}(t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}+t_{2}^{3}t_{3}t_{4}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,32}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,33}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}t_{3}^{4}t_{4}^{3}&=Sq^{2}(t_{1}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}^{3})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,28}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}t_{3}^{5}t_{4}^{2}&=Sq^{2}(t_{1}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,34}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}t_{2}^{4}t_{3}^{3}&=Sq^{2}(t_{1}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{3})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,30}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}t_{2}^{5}t_{3}^{2}&=Sq^{2}(t_{1}t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,36}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}^{2}t_{2}t_{4}^{5}&=Sq^{1}(t_{1}t_{2}t_{4}^{5})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,23}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,29}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}^{2}t_{2}t_{3}^{5}&=Sq^{1}(t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}^{5})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,24}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,30}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}^{4}t_{2}^{3}t_{4}&=Sq^{1}(t_{1}t_{2}^{5}t_{4})+Sq^{2}(t_{1}^{2}t_{2}^{3}t_{4}+t_{1}t_{2}^{3}t_{4}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,25}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,35}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}^{4}t_{2}^{3}t_{3}&=Sq^{1}(t_{1}t_{2}^{5}t_{3})+Sq^{2}(t_{1}^{2}t_{2}^{3}t_{3}+t_{1}t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,26}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,36}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}^{6}t_{2}t_{4}&=Sq^{1}(t_{1}^{5}t_{2}t_{4})+Sq^{2}(t_{1}^{3}t_{2}^{2}t_{4}+t_{1}^{3}t_{2}t_{4}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,38}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,41}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}),\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptt_{1}^{6}t_{2}t_{3}&=Sq^{1}(t_{1}^{5}t_{2}t_{3})+Sq^{2}(t_{1}^{3}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}+t_{1}^{3}t_{2}t_{3}^{2})+{\rm adm}_{1,\,39}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,42}\mod(\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}).\end{array}

Combining the above computations and the fact that
\theta_{i}(f)+f\equiv 0
for
1\leq i\leq 3,
we obtain

\gamma_{j}=0
for
j\in\mathbb{J}_{1}=\{27,\ldots,31,34,35,36\}
and
\gamma_{j}=\gamma_{19}
for
j\not\in\mathbb{J}_{1}.
This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.1.2
.

There is a direct summand decomposition of the
S_{4}
-submodules:

\widehat{{\rm Ker}_{n_{1}}}=S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,43})\bigoplus S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,46}),

where

\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptS_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,43})&=\langle[{\rm adm}_{1,\,43}],[{\rm adm}_{1,\,44}],[{\rm adm}_{1,\,45}]\rangle,\ \mbox{with }\\
&\quad{\rm adm}_{1,\,43}=t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}^{4},\,{\rm adm}_{1,\,44}=t_{1}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}t_{4}^{4},\,{\rm adm}_{1,\,45}=t_{1}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{4}t_{4},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptS_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,46})&=\langle[{\rm adm}_{1,\,46}],[{\rm adm}_{1,\,47}],[{\rm adm}_{1,\,48}],[{\rm adm}_{1,\,49}]\rangle\ \mbox{with}\\
&\quad{\rm adm}_{1,\,46}=t_{1}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}^{3},\,{\rm adm}_{1,\,47}=t_{1}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{3}t_{4}^{2},\,{\rm adm}_{1,\,48}=t_{1}t_{2}^{3}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}^{2},\,{\rm adm}_{1,\,48}=t_{1}^{3}t_{2}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}^{2}.\end{array}

Moreover,
[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,43})]^{S_{4}}=\langle[p_{1,\,4}]\rangle
with
p_{1,\,4}:=\sum_{43\leq j\leq 45}{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}
and
[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,46})]^{S_{4}}=0.

Proof.

It is known, from a result of Sum
[
43
,
44
]
, that the set
\{[{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}]:\,43\leq j\leq 49\}
is an admissible monomial basis of
\widehat{{\rm Ker}_{n_{1}}}.
So, it is easy to see that
\widehat{{\rm Ker}_{n_{1}}}\cong S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,43})\bigoplus S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,46}).

Now if
[g]\in[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,43})]^{S_{4}}
and
[h]\in[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{1,\,46})]^{S_{4}},
then

g\equiv\sum_{43\leq j\leq 45}\gamma_{j}{\rm adm}_{1,\,j},\ \ h\equiv\sum_{46\leq j\leq 49}\gamma_{j}{\rm adm}_{1,\,j},

where
\gamma_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}/2.
For each
1\leq i\leq 3,
direct calculating the equalities
\theta_{i}(g)
and
\theta_{i}(h)
in the terms
{\rm adm}_{1,\,j}
for
43\leq j\leq 49
mod (
\overline{\mathcal{A}}P^{\otimes 4}_{n_{1}}
), and using the relations
\theta_{i}(g)+g\equiv 0
and
\theta_{i}(h)+h\equiv 0,
we get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{1}(g)+g&\equiv\gamma_{43}({\rm adm}_{1,\,44}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,45})\equiv 0,\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{2}(g)+g&\equiv(\gamma_{43}+\gamma_{44})({\rm adm}_{1,\,43}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,44})\equiv 0,\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{3}(g)+g&\equiv(\gamma_{44}+\gamma_{45})({\rm adm}_{1,\,43}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,44})\equiv 0,\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{1}(h)+h&\equiv(\gamma_{46}+\gamma_{47}){\rm adm}_{1,\,43}+(\gamma_{48}+\gamma_{49})({\rm adm}_{1,\,48}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,49})\equiv 0,\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{2}(h)+h&\equiv\gamma_{49}({\rm adm}_{1,\,43}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,44})+(\gamma_{47}+\gamma_{48})({\rm adm}_{1,\,47}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,48})\equiv 0,\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\theta_{3}(h)+h&\equiv(\gamma_{46}+\gamma_{47})({\rm adm}_{1,\,46}+{\rm adm}_{1,\,47})\equiv 0.\end{array}

From the equalities above, we get
\gamma_{43}=\gamma_{44}=\gamma_{45}
and
\gamma_{j}=0
for
46\leq j\leq 49.
The lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 3.1.3
.

For each
s\geq 2,
we have
\dim[\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n_{s}}}]^{S_{4}}\leq 6.

Proof.

By direct computations using the admissible basis of
\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n_{s}}}
above, we notice that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,1})=\langle\{[{\rm adm}_{s,\,j}]:\,1\leq j\leq 12\}\rangle,\\
&S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,13},\ {\rm adm}_{s,\,37},\ {\rm adm}_{s,\,59},\ {\rm adm}_{s,\,63})=\langle\{[{\rm adm}_{s,\,j}]:\,j\in\mathbb{J}\}\rangle,\ \mbox{where}\\
&\quad\mathbb{J}=\{13,\ldots,24,37,\ldots,48,55,\ldots,66\},\\
&S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,25})=\langle\{[{\rm adm}_{s,\,j}]:\,25\leq j\leq 36\}\rangle,\ \ S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,49})=\langle\{[{\rm adm}_{s,\,j}]:\,49\leq j\leq 54\}\rangle,\\
\end{array}

are
S_{4}
-submodules of
\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n_{s}}}.
Hence, we have an isomorphism

\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n_{s}}}\cong S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,1})\bigoplus S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,13},{\rm adm}_{s,\,37},{\rm adm}_{s,\,59},{\rm adm}_{s,\,63})\bigoplus S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,25})\bigoplus S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,49}).

Then, by the similar techniques as in the proof of Lemma
3.1.1
, it may be concluded that

i)

[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,1})]^{S_{4}}=\langle[p_{s,\,1}]\rangle
with
p_{s,\,1}:=\sum_{1\leq j\leq 12}{\rm adm}_{s,\,j}

ii)

\begin{array}[]{ll}&[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,13},{\rm adm}_{s,\,37},{\rm adm}_{s,\,59},{\rm adm}_{s,\,63})\bigoplus S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,25})]^{S_{4}}\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\langle[p_{2,\,2}+p_{2,\,3}+p_{2,\,4}+p_{2,\,5}],[p_{2,\,3}+p_{2,\,4}+p_{2,\,6}],[p_{2,\,2}+p_{2,\,7}]\rangle&\mbox{if $s=2$},\\
\langle[p_{s,\,2}+p_{s,\,3}],[p_{s,\,2}+p_{s,\,4}],[p_{s,\,2}+p_{s,\,5}]\rangle&\mbox{if $s\geq 3$},\end{array}\right.\end{array}

where

\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptp_{2,\,2}&={\rm adm}_{2,\,13}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,16}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,18}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,19},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptp_{2,\,3}&={\rm adm}_{2,\,14}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,17}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,20}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,21},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptp_{2,\,4}&={\rm adm}_{2,\,15}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,22}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,23}+{\rm adm}_{2,\,24},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptp_{2,\,5}&=\sum_{37\leq j\leq 48}{\rm adm}_{2,\,j},\ \ p_{2,\,6}=\sum_{55\leq j\leq 58}{\rm adm}_{2,\,j}+\sum_{63\leq j\leq 66}{\rm adm}_{2,\,j},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptp_{2,\,7}&=\sum_{59\leq j\leq 62}{\rm adm}_{2,\,j},\ \ p_{s,\,2}=\sum_{13\leq j\leq 24}{\rm adm}_{s,\,j},\ \ p_{s,\,3}=\sum_{37\leq j\leq 48}{\rm adm}_{s,\,j},\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0ptp_{s,\,4}&=\sum_{55\leq j\leq 58}{\rm adm}_{s,\,j},\ \ p_{s,\,5}=\sum_{59\leq j\leq 66}{\rm adm}_{s,\,j}.\end{array}

iii)

[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,25})]^{S_{4}}=\langle[p_{s,\,6}]\rangle
with
p_{s,\,6}:=\sum_{25\leq j\leq 36}{\rm adm}_{s,\,j}.

iv)

[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,49})]^{S_{4}}=\langle[p_{s,\,7}]\rangle
with
p_{s,\,7}:=\sum_{49\leq j\leq 54}{\rm adm}_{s,\,j}.

These imply that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\dim[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,j})]^{S_{4}}=1\ \mbox{for}\ j=1,\,25,\,49,\\
&\dim[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,13},{\rm adm}_{s,\,37},{\rm adm}_{s,\,59},{\rm adm}_{s,\,63})]^{S_{4}}=3.\end{array}

Now, the lemma follows from the fact that

\begin{array}[]{ll}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\dim[\underline{{\rm Ker}_{n_{s}}}]^{S_{4}}&\leq\dim[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,1})]^{S_{4}}+\dim[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,13},{\rm adm}_{s,\,37},{\rm adm}_{s,\,59},{\rm adm}_{s,\,63})]^{S_{4}}\\
&\quad+\dim[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,25})]^{S_{4}}+\dim\dim[S_{4}({\rm adm}_{s,\,49})]^{S_{4}}.\end{array}

∎
